_49e095fc"><span class="_384a5d29 _00834456 _8443bf41 _9d5c2e21 eaede7cf _499f3975 c6589e85 _7c35b94f _1641077c _2fdff1b4 _5d395fc5 _4d4bfd45 f0fb3710 _49d4eb13 _7391903f _7ae9a367 _18847223 fb1ae6b8">Follow</span></div></span></button></div></div></div></div><p class="_384a5d29 _7c35b94f _6fa6a8b1 _290f5b77 _337af43d _67bcbbcf _8bff2c26 _9367426d _352c13d8 d9fcd881 _9c9aefe4 _00e11fba _49e095fc ddae5c29" componentkey="f192fb56-b368-46d2-ab27-a7985c7d99e8"><span class="d17a3034 f0fb3710 _49d4eb13 _7391903f _7ae9a367 _18847223 _4e9465fc _36064fe8" tabindex="-1" data-testid="expandable-text-box"><br>The biggest AI company in the world just told us what they think the real bottleneck is. It's not the models. It's getting humans to actually use them.<br><br><a class="d7aa8400 _7d2088df" href="https://www.linkedin.com/company/openai/" tabindex="-1"><span class="abd68f3e"><strong>OpenAI</strong></span></a><span class="_836eec6c"> </span>launched a $4B consulting arm. They bought Tomoro for 150 deployment engineers, brought in TPG, Bain Capital, and Brookfield as co-leads, and pulled McKinsey, Bain &amp; Company, and Capgemini in as investors. The plan is to embed engineers inside enterprises and rebuild workflows around AI from the ground up.<br><br>The frontier lab thinks the gap between capability and value is so wide that solving it needs $4B and McKinsey.<br><br>I agree with the diagnosis. The models are already capable of doing real work for most people I talk to. The barrier is that nobody taught us how to work with something that reasons. Human psychology around AI is the missing layer. Most people are still typing into a chatbox like it's Google when they could be handing it the whole job.<br><br>This is also where I land on what the Chutes team is building. Open models, decentralized inference, pay per request instead of per token, the whole thing pointed at giving more people access to capable AI without routing it all through one company's roadmap. Working on the opposite end of the spectrum from what OpenAI shipped today feels good. Both ends of the market need to exist.<br><br>The part I keep coming back to: the limiting factor on AI helping your friends, your family, your team, your business is not the model. It is whether someone shows you how to use it. That role matters more than the infra fight everyone is watching.<br><br>We are early. _6fa6a8b1 _290f5b77 _337af43d _67bcbbcf _8bff2c26 _9367426d _352c13d8 d9fcd881 _9c9aefe4 _00e11fba _49e095fc ddae5c29" componentkey="078a1760-c0f0-4fbf-852f-8ddf7b5fd274"><span class="d17a3034 f0fb3710 _49d4eb13 _7391903f _7ae9a367 _18847223 _4e9465fc _36064fe8" tabindex="-1" data-testid="expandable-text-box">Most engineers are average. That's how averages work.<br><br>We trained the most powerful code generation tools on their output. Then we were surprised when the output was mediocre.<br><br>I published an article blaming AI models for ignoring my<span class="_836eec6c"> </span><a class="d7aa8400 _7d2088df" href="https://www.linkedin.com/safety/go/?url=http%3A%2F%2FCLAUDE%2Emd&amp;urlhash=onXc&amp;mt=j-XQ-zx8_Na4gF6Qgm2yoGK-nMZydH-WQXe81tO0uhI3kd0VeWN7rrL-ij6K4lMVn6oYYRo7e2Ljf2sXxI4mM60nvLrUqVefSWXpRnN0et6R9TXinsF5cngRGIo&amp;isSdui=true" target="_blank" tabindex="-1"><span class="abd68f3e"><strong>CLAUDE.md</strong></span></a><span class="_836eec6c"> </span>rules. Then I dug deeper and realized I was wrong about who to blame.<br><br>The model isn't disobeying. It's doing statistics. My clean architecture is one signal. The training data contains millions of repos where `as any`, skipped tests, and copy-paste are the norm. For the model, my code is the outlier. The slop is the baseline.<br><br>The data backs this up:<br>- 518M GitHub projects, vast majority abandoned or unreviewed<br>- Refactoring collapsed from 25% to under 10% (GitClear, 211M lines)<br>- AI-generated code contains 1.7x more defects (CodeRabbit, 470 PRs)<br>- 96% of developers don't trust AI code, yet only 48% check it before committing (Sonar, 1,149 devs)<br><br>You can't fix this with better prompts. Text competing against training data is a losing strategy. You're bringing a prompt to a probability fight.<br><br>The only thing that works is code against code. Hooks. Linters. CI gates. Deterministic enforcement, not probabilistic suggestions.<br><br>The 10x is not real. The data is real.<br><br>Full analysis with Nature, CMU, and GitClear research:<br><a class="d7aa8400 _7d2088df" _6fa6a8b1 _290f5b77 _337af43d _67bcbbcf _8bff2c26 _9367426d _352c13d8 d9fcd881 _9c9aefe4 _00e11fba _49e095fc ddae5c29" componentkey="0c208b65-9d30-4c56-9be0-ea6b7cc69abe"><span class="d17a3034 f0fb3710 _49d4eb13 _7391903f _7ae9a367 _18847223 _4e9465fc _36064fe8" tabindex="-1" data-testid="expandable-text-box">Most robots never survive contact with the real world.<br><br>Every decade, a new wave of robotics companies builds something impressive. It works in a controlled environment, raises capital, gets attention. And then deployment starts, and everything changes.<br>Because robotics has never been limited by technology. It has been limited by execution.<br><br>There is now a term for this. The “physical AI deployment gap,” the widening difference between what works in a demo and what can operate reliably at scale in the real world (source<span class="_836eec6c"> </span><a class="d7aa8400 _7d2088df" href="https://www.linkedin.com/safety/go/?url=https%3A%2F%2Flnkd%2Ein%2FgaTA3eiB&amp;urlhash=Ld7q&amp;mt=KpoxeuR3PzcEBbmb0orU7jyQaOfFbT1_FYGP-lPsY28zueU64HtDZe5XiGSo69u-vl14bI4oX0UkbF_EhKwmQyFBOkV8JTCoCG9iLmV6pXWJPT6K-yuKcTC3x78&amp;isSdui=true" target="_blank" tabindex="-1"><span class="abd68f3e"><strong>https://lnkd.in/gaTA3eiB</strong></span></a>).<br><br>Henry Ford once said, “You can’t build a reputation on what you are going to do.” That has quietly defined this industry for years.<br><br>It is one thing to get a robot moving in a warehouse in San Francisco. It is another thing entirely to ship that machine across the country at a reasonable cost, get it onto an active construction site, train a crew that has never seen it before, and support that operation in real time without slowing anything down.<br><br>What most people miss is that this gap is not a temporary delay. It is structural. It comes from the mismatch between controlled environments and the unpredictability of real job sites, where reliability, integration, and maintainability matter more than peak performance.<br><br>The moment you deploy robots in the real world, you are no longer just a robotics company. You become a logistics company, a service company, and an operations company all at once.<br><br>The graveyard of robotics startups is full of teams that built something impressive but never solved this layer. Systems that look flawless in demos struggle when exposed to real environments, because demos optimize for capability, while real operations demand consistency over time.<br><br>At BotCrew, this is where most of our focus has gone. And I want to give real credit to our logistics, demo, and deployment teams 🦾  They are the reason this works. They move machines across active sites nationwide, make sure our customers can rely on them in the field.<br><br>We spend a lot of time on job sites across the country, and to date we have not come across another robot operating at that level in the field.<br>This is not a technology story. It is an execution story.<br><br>And execution is what turns robotics from a prototype into infrastructure.<br><br><a class="d7aa8400 _7d2088df"
